- open_blockers
- waived_issues
- risk_score
- gate_decision: PASS | WARN | BLOCK | NEED_REVIEW

7. End-to-end workflow#

Workflow 1: CI failure triage#

text
1. Developer merges PR or starts release candidate build.
2. CI/CD runs tests.
3. Some tests fail.
4. CI emits TestFailureEvent to Kafka/PubSub.
5. FailureEvent Normalizer enriches it with:
   - commit diff
   - owner info
   - test history
   - previous pass/fail trend
   - artifact links
6. Artifact Processor parses logs and extracts relevant stack traces / error windows.
7. Search service retrieves similar past failures.
8. AI RCA Orchestrator generates:
   - concise summary
   - suspected component
   - likely regression or flaky test
   - evidence links
   - confidence score
9. Triage UI shows result to engineer.
10. Engineer accepts/corrects the result.
11. Feedback goes into Eval Store and future training/evaluation set.

Workflow 2: Large-scale simulation release qualification#

text
1. Release candidate triggers simulation batch.
2. Millions of scenarios run across distributed workers.
3. Results stream into data pipeline.
4. Aggregator computes scenario-level and cluster-level stats:
   - pass/fail
   - safety metric violations
   - planner disengagement
   - perception mismatch
   - collision / near miss
   - route failure
5. Failure clustering groups similar failures.
6. LLM only analyzes representative cluster samples, not every failed scenario.
7. RCA Orchestrator creates cluster-level hypotheses.
8. Release Gate Service combines:
   - deterministic safety rules
   - regression deltas
   - severity
   - coverage
   - RCA confidence
9. Dashboard shows release readiness and blockers.

Workflow 3: On-road incident review#

text
1. Vehicle/on-road system records notable event.
2. Event metadata and artifact references are ingested.
3. Video/keyframe pipeline extracts relevant frames.
4. Metrics/logs around event window are aligned by timestamp.
5. Multimodal analyzer summarizes:
   - what happened
   - what system perceived
   - what system planned
   - what human/operator did
   - whether behavior matches known issue
6. Triage UI shows event timeline and RCA hypotheses.
7. Human triager confirms label and assigns owner.

8. AI/LLM design: 不要做成 naive chatbot#

这里是面试重点。

8.1 AI Orchestrator pipeline#

text
FailureEvent
   │
   ▼
Deterministic Pre-check
   - known error signature
   - flaky test database
   - missing artifact
   - infra outage
   │
   ▼
Context Builder
   - logs around failure window
   - metrics anomaly summary
   - commit diff summary
   - similar incidents
   - runbook snippets
   - owner mapping
   │
   ▼
RAG Retriever
   - previous Jira tickets
   - incident reviews
   - release notes
   - test documentation
   - code ownership
   │
   ▼
Model Router
   - cheap text model for simple log failures
   - stronger LLM for complex RCA
   - vision/video model for image/video-heavy events
   │
   ▼
RCA Generator
   - summary
   - hypotheses
   - evidence
   - confidence
   - recommended next steps
   │
   ▼
Verifier
   - schema validation
   - citation/evidence check
   - policy check
   - hallucination guard

8.2 为什么先 deterministic rules，再 LLM？#

因为很多 release triage 问题不需要 LLM：

known flaky test · infra timeout · missing dependency · branch config mismatch · simulator environment outage · duplicated known issue · obvious assertion failure

这些用 rule / classifier 更便宜、更稳定。LLM 适合：

多信号综合解释 · 长 logs summarization · similar incident comparison · human-readable RCA report · complex scenario reasoning · ambiguous failure clustering

你可以说：

我不会让 LLM 成为 first-line gatekeeper。我的设计是 deterministic rules first, ML/LLM second, human review for high-risk decisions。

9. Multimodal data handling#

岗位明确提到 logs、metrics、test results、images、video，所以你一定要讲 multimodal pipeline。

Logs#

处理方式：

parse structured logs · extract error windows · stack trace normalization · log template mining · severity filtering · component tagging · chunk + embedding · link every summary back to line range / timestamp

Metrics#

处理方式：

align metrics by event timestamp · anomaly detection · compare baseline vs candidate release · detect regression trend · generate metric summary for LLM

例如：

text
Before:
Raw metric time series with 10,000 points.

After:
"Planner latency p95 increased from 82ms to 147ms after commit abc123.
Spike starts 12s before route deviation. Similar pattern seen in INC-2391."

Images / Video#

不要把整段 video 丢给 LLM。应该：

text
Video
  │
  ├── shot/keyframe extraction
  ├── object/event detection
  ├── timestamp alignment with logs
  ├── selected clip generation
  ├── frame captions / embeddings
  └── multimodal model summary

重点是：

only analyze relevant time window · sample representative keyframes · align video timestamp with planner/perception logs · store raw video in Object Storage · store captions/embeddings/metadata in searchable index

10. Confidence scoring 设计#

这是这个岗位的高价值点。

LLM 输出不能只说“我觉得”。要有 confidence signal。

Confidence score 可以由多部分组成#

text
Final confidence =
  0.30 * evidence_strength
+ 0.20 * historical_match_score
+ 0.20 * model_self_consistency
+ 0.15 * signal_agreement
+ 0.10 * data_completeness
+ 0.05 * human_feedback_prior

解释：

evidence_strength：是否有明确 logs / metrics / video timestamp 支撑 · historical_match_score：和过去 incident 是否高度相似 · model_self_consistency：多次推理 / 多模型是否一致 · signal_agreement：logs、metrics、test result、video 是否指向同一组件 · data_completeness：artifact 是否完整 · human_feedback_prior：过去类似判断是否被 triager 接受

输出格式示例#

text
Hypothesis 1:
Perception module regression caused missed object detection.

Confidence: 0.78

Evidence:
- Object detection confidence dropped from 0.91 to 0.34 at T+4.2s.
- Same scenario passed in previous release candidate.
- Similar incident INC-1842 was caused by perception model config change.
- Recent commit abc123 changed perception threshold config.

Counter-evidence:
- Planner latency also increased, so planner-side contribution is possible.

这个格式非常适合面试。

11. Release gate 不能完全靠 LLM#

这个必须强调。

Release Gate Service#

text
Inputs:
- test pass rate
- scenario coverage
- critical safety metric violations
- new regression clusters
- severity distribution
- open blocker count
- known issue waivers
- LLM RCA confidence
- human triage status

Outputs:
- PASS
- WARN
- BLOCK
- NEED_REVIEW

Gate policy example#

text
BLOCK if:
- any critical safety scenario newly fails
- regression cluster affects high-priority ODD
- blocker bug has no owner
- required simulation coverage below threshold
- incident severity >= S1 and not waived

WARN if:
- LLM detects likely regression but confidence < threshold
- flaky test rate increases
- non-critical failures exceed trend baseline

PASS if:
- deterministic gates pass
- no new severe regression
- all high-risk failures triaged or waived

你可以说：

LLM 可以 inform release decision，但不应该 be the release decision.

Release gate 需要 deterministic, auditable, reproducible policy。

12. Evaluation: 面试里一定要主动讲#

很多候选人只会讲 RAG，不会讲 eval。这个岗位 nice-to-have 明确提到 model evaluation, confidence scoring, explainability。

Offline evaluation#

构建 golden dataset：

text
Historical incidents:
- input artifacts
- human RCA
- final root cause
- owner
- fix commit
- severity
- whether it was regression

Metrics:

text
- RCA top-1 accuracy
- RCA top-3 accuracy
- owner prediction accuracy
- duplicate detection precision/recall
- regression classification precision/recall
- hallucination rate
- citation/evidence correctness
- summary usefulness score
- triage time reduction

Online evaluation#

text
- triager accepted suggestion rate
- time to first hypothesis
- time to owner assignment
- time to resolution
- false confidence rate
- release decision override rate
- human correction distribution

A/B testing#

可以 A/B：

baseline manual triage · AI summary only · AI RCA + similar incidents · AI RCA + confidence + recommended owner

目标不是“LLM 准不准”这么粗，而是：

Does the system reduce triage latency without increasing release risk?

13. Feedback loop#

text
Human Feedback
   │
   ├── correct root cause
   ├── wrong hypothesis
   ├── missing evidence
   ├── duplicate of known issue
   ├── bad owner
   ├── hallucinated detail
   └── useful summary
        │
        ▼
Eval Dataset / Prompt Improvement / Fine-tuning Candidate / Rule Update

你要强调：

human feedback 先进入 eval，不是直接 online training · high-risk domain 不能随便自动学习 · prompt / model / rule changes 要 versioned · 每次模型升级要 replay historical dataset

14. Where to put Redis / cache#

这个岗位下 Redis 很自然，而且应该放多个位置：

text
1. Hot triage queue cache
   - latest failure status
   - dashboard counters
   - active release health snapshot

2. LLM response cache
   - same failure_signature + same artifacts + same model_version
   - avoid repeated expensive analysis

3. Retrieval cache
   - similar incident search results
   - frequently accessed runbooks

4. Rate limiting / job coordination
   - per-team LLM quota
   - avoid one release batch overwhelming model service

5. UI session cache
   - user filters
   - dashboard materialized views

但也要说清楚：

Redis is not source of truth. Source of truth remains Metadata Store / Data Lake / Eval Store. Redis is for hot path latency and cost control.

15. Detailed component design#

15.1 Ingestion Service#

Responsibilities:

receive events from CI/CD, simulation, on-road pipelines · validate schema · deduplicate · assign event_id · enrich metadata · publish normalized FailureEvent

Design choices:

Kafka/PubSub for streaming · Schema Registry for compatibility · dead-letter queue for malformed events · idempotent writes

15.2 Artifact Processor#

Responsibilities:

fetch logs/images/videos · extract useful segments · redact sensitive data · generate summaries and embeddings · store processed artifacts

Async workers:

text
LogWorker
MetricWorker
TraceWorker
ImageWorker
VideoWorker
EmbeddingWorker

Why async?

video processing is slow · LLM calls are expensive · CI/CD should not block on deep RCA

15.3 Search / Retrieval Service#

Supports:

keyword search · vector search · hybrid search · metadata filters

Queries:

text
similar failures where:
- same test_case_id
- same failure_signature
- same component
- similar log embedding
- same scenario cluster
- same metric anomaly pattern

15.4 AI RCA Orchestrator#

Key design:

workflow engine / state machine · model router · prompt templates · evidence validator · output schema validation · retry / fallback

Example state machine:

text
NEW_EVENT
  -> WAIT_FOR_ARTIFACTS
  -> RUN_RULES
  -> RETRIEVE_CONTEXT
  -> GENERATE_RCA
  -> VERIFY_OUTPUT
  -> PUBLISH_RESULT
  -> WAIT_FOR_FEEDBACK

15.5 Triage UI#

Features:

  • release health overview
  • failure clusters
  • severity filter
  • owner filter
  • evidence timeline
  • AI summary
  • similar incidents
  • confidence score
  • human feedback buttons
  • Jira/Slack integration

UI should not hide evidence. It should make evidence easy to inspect.

15.6 Dashboard / Reporting#

Dashboard views:

text
Release Candidate View:
- overall readiness
- pass/fail trend
- critical blockers
- new regressions
- known issues
- simulation coverage
- risk score

Component View:
- failures by module
- owner load
- recurring issues
- flaky tests

Trend View:
- release-over-release health
- triage latency
- RCA accuracy
- model usefulness

16. Failure clustering / regression detection#

这是 release triage 的核心。

Clustering signals#

text
failure_signature =
  normalized stack trace
+ test_case_id
+ scenario_id
+ component
+ log template
+ metric anomaly pattern
+ video/event caption embedding

Regression detection logic#

text
A failure is likely regression if:
- it did not appear in previous release candidate
- it appears after a specific commit range
- it clusters with failures introduced recently
- baseline simulation passed before
- metric distribution shifted significantly

Commit correlation#

text
Input:
- first bad build
- last good build
- commit range
- changed files
- code owners
- test ownership
- component dependency graph

Output:
- likely suspect commits
- likely owners
- confidence

这个可以自然连接你的 CI/CD 和 deployment experience。

17. Responsible AI / safety#

wolf 公开案例里也有 generative AI 辅助 safety-standard code fixes，并强调 reasoning 和 certainty 让工程师有信心采用建议；这个和本岗位“AI-driven analysis with confidence signals”很一致。([Microsoft][3])

面试里你要说：

  1. No black-box release decision

LLM 不直接 approve release

  1. Evidence-backed output

每个 hypothesis 必须有 evidence_refs

  1. Human-in-the-loop

high severity / low confidence 需要 human review

  1. Auditability

log model version, prompt version, input context, output, reviewer action

  1. Data minimization

不把完整 raw logs/video/code 全部发给模型

  1. Access control

team-based ACL · code/log visibility based on permission

  1. Hallucination guard

output schema · citation check · “unknown” is allowed · no unsupported claims

18. Security design#

text
Security controls:
- SSO / IAM integration
- RBAC by team / project / release
- artifact-level ACL
- secret / token redaction
- PII redaction from logs/video metadata
- audit log for all AI analysis access
- encryption at rest and in transit
- model provider boundary control
- internal model or VPC/private endpoint for sensitive data

Key sentence:

In a safety-critical automotive environment, AI tooling must follow least privilege and data minimization. The RCA report should expose enough evidence for debugging, but not leak unrelated logs, code, or sensitive driving data.

19. Observability#

你要给 observability 三层：

System observability#

text
- ingestion lag
- processing latency
- queue depth
- worker error rate
- artifact fetch failure
- LLM latency
- LLM error rate
- cache hit rate

AI quality observability#

text
- accepted RCA rate
- corrected RCA rate
- hallucination reports
- confidence calibration
- top-3 owner accuracy
- duplicate detection precision/recall

Release workflow observability#

text
- time to triage
- time to owner assignment
- time to resolution
- blocker aging
- release gate override rate
- flaky test trend

20. Cost and performance optimization#

LLM-heavy systems容易烧钱。你要主动讲 cost control。

text
Cost controls:
1. Pre-filter simple known failures with rules.
2. Summarize logs before LLM.
3. Analyze representative cluster samples, not every failure.
4. Cache RCA for same failure signature.
5. Use small model for summarization, large model for complex RCA.
6. Use batch embedding jobs.
7. Process video only for high-severity or novel clusters.
8. Limit context by retrieval relevance.
9. Apply per-team quota and priority queue.

Model routing example:

text
Simple known CI failure:
  rule engine + small LLM summary

Complex simulation regression:
  retrieval + large LLM + confidence scorer

On-road video incident:
  keyframe extraction + vision model + text LLM RCA

High-severity release blocker:
  multi-pass analysis + human review

21. Failure modes and mitigation#

21.1 LLM hallucinates root cause#

Mitigation:

require evidence_refs · verifier checks references exist · confidence penalty for unsupported claims · UI shows “hypothesis, not confirmed root cause”

21.2 Missing artifacts#

Mitigation:

data completeness score · mark RCA as low confidence · retry artifact fetch · alert infra owner

21.3 Huge simulation burst overwhelms system#

Mitigation:

async queue · backpressure · cluster first, analyze samples · priority by severity/release stage · quota per release

21.4 Similar incident retrieval returns wrong context#

Mitigation:

hybrid search · metadata filters · reranker · show retrieved context to user · feedback loop

21.5 Model upgrade changes behavior#

Mitigation:

model versioning · prompt versioning · offline replay · canary rollout · rollback

21.6 Bad confidence calibration#

Mitigation:

calibration dataset · reliability curve · separate confidence model · compare predicted confidence vs human correctness

22. Interview deep dive: release readiness score#

可以这样设计：

text
ReleaseRiskScore =
  weighted sum of:
  - critical failure count
  - new regression cluster severity
  - simulation coverage gap
  - flaky test trend
  - unresolved blocker age
  - affected component criticality
  - confidence-adjusted RCA risk
  - historical release stability

Example:

text
Risk score 0-100:
0-30: low risk
31-60: needs review
61-80: high risk
81-100: block release

但要强调：

The score is not the final decision. It is an explainable aggregation to help release engineers focus attention.

23. API examples#

Create / ingest failure event#

http
POST /v1/failure-events
json
{
  "source_type": "SIMULATION",
  "release_id": "rel-2026-07-06-rc1",
  "build_id": "build-123",
  "commit_sha": "abc123",
  "test_suite": "planner_regression_suite",
  "scenario_id": "scenario-456",
  "severity": "S2",
  "artifact_refs": {
    "logs": ["s3://logs/build-123/scenario-456.log"],
    "metrics": ["s3://metrics/build-123/scenario-456.parquet"],
    "video": ["s3://videos/scenario-456.mp4"]
  }
}

Get RCA result#

http
GET /v1/failure-events/{event_id}/rca
json
{
  "summary": "Planner failed to yield in an unprotected left-turn scenario.",
  "hypotheses": [
    {
      "component": "planner",
      "hypothesis": "Recent cost function change increased preference for forward progress over yielding.",
      "confidence": 0.74,
      "evidence_refs": [
        "metric:planner_cost.forward_progress@T+3.2s",
        "commit:abc123",
        "similar_incident:INC-2198"
      ],
      "counter_evidence": [
        "Perception tracked the vehicle correctly with stable confidence."
      ]
    }
  ],
  "recommended_actions": [
    "Assign to planner owner.",
    "Compare cost function output with previous release.",
    "Replay scenario with commit range abc120..abc123."
  ]
}

Get release readiness#

http
GET /v1/releases/{release_id}/readiness
json
{
  "release_id": "rel-2026-07-06-rc1",
  "gate_decision": "NEED_REVIEW",
  "risk_score": 67,
  "new_regression_clusters": 4,
  "critical_blockers": 1,
  "simulation_coverage": 0.93,
  "summary": "Release candidate shows one new high-severity planner regression cluster."
}

24. 45-minute system design answer structure#

你可以按这个时间分配：

0–5 min: clarify#

问：

Are we designing for CI failures, simulation failures, on-road events, or all? · Is the goal decision support or automatic release gating? · What data sources are available? · What latency is expected? · Are we allowed to use external LLM APIs, or only internal models? · How critical is video/image analysis in v1?

然后主动 scope：

I’ll design v1 for CI + simulation failure triage, with extensibility for on-road multimodal events.

5–10 min: requirements + BOTE#

说 functional / non-functional / scale。

10–20 min: architecture#

画前面那张图。

20–30 min: deep dive AI RCA#

讲：

deterministic rules first · RAG · multimodal preprocessing · confidence scoring · evidence validation · human feedback

30–37 min: release gate + eval#

讲：

release readiness score · gate policy · offline/online eval · model governance

37–42 min: reliability / security / cost#

讲：

async processing · cache/Redis · fallback · ACL · audit · cost controls

42–45 min: tradeoffs#

总结：

The key tradeoff is speed vs accuracy vs cost. For release workflows, I optimize for evidence-backed, auditable decision support rather than fully autonomous decisions.

25. 常见 follow-up 题#

Q1: Why not just feed all logs into an LLM?#

回答：

因为 raw logs 太大、太贵、噪音太多，而且可能包含 sensitive data。更好的做法是：

parse and filter logs · extract relevant time window · normalize error signatures · retrieve historical incidents · send compact context to LLM · require evidence-backed output

Q2: How do you prevent hallucination?#

回答：

output schema · evidence_refs required · verifier checks evidence existence · confidence score · human review for high-risk cases · allow “unknown” · audit model/prompt/input/output · offline eval before rollout

Q3: How do you evaluate RCA quality?#

回答：

用 historical incidents 做 golden dataset。指标包括：

top-1 / top-3 RCA accuracy · owner prediction accuracy · duplicate detection precision/recall · hallucination rate · evidence correctness · triager acceptance rate · triage time reduction

Q4: How do you handle video?#

回答：

不直接把 full video 送进模型。先做：

event window selection · keyframe extraction · timestamp alignment · object/event captions · embeddings · only send representative frames/clips to multimodal model

Q5: What if LLM is down?#

回答：

CI/CD 和 release gate 不能依赖 LLM availability。系统 fallback：

deterministic rules still run · dashboard shows raw failure and known signatures · RCA marked pending · queue retries later · high-severity issues go to manual triage

Q6: How do you integrate with CI/CD?#

回答：

CI/CD emits structured events after each build/test. The platform ingests events, enriches with commit/test/artifact metadata, analyzes asynchronously, and posts summary back to CI UI, Slack, Jira, and release dashboard. Release gate uses deterministic policy plus triage status, not raw LLM text.

26. 你可以怎么 sell 自己#

结合你的背景，可以这样讲：

My background is a strong match because I have built internal engineering platforms around vehicle/fleet operations, deployment workflows, CI/CD reliability, monitoring, and real-time operational signals. In this role, I would bring that platform mindset into AI-driven release and triage tooling: make the system reliable first, integrate deeply with engineers’ workflow, and use LLMs where they improve triage speed while preserving traceability and human control.

更具体一点：

At Cruise/GM-style environments, I worked on fleet management, deployment, telemetry, health monitoring, and CI/CD-adjacent tooling. That gives me practical intuition about what engineers need during release: not a generic chatbot, but a workflow-integrated system that connects artifacts, status, ownership, and evidence.

27. 最适合你练的 5 个 system design prompts#

Prompt 1#

Design an AI-powered triage system for failed autonomous driving simulation tests.

重点：

simulation result ingestion · failure clustering · artifact processing · RCA with RAG · release risk dashboard

Prompt 2#

Design a release qualification dashboard for AD/ADAS software.

重点：

release health aggregation · gate policy · trend analysis · blocker management · auditability

Prompt 3#

Design an LLM-based root cause analysis assistant for CI/CD failures.

重点：

logs/test results/code diffs · similar failures · owner prediction · confidence scoring · feedback loop

Prompt 4#

Design a multimodal incident review platform for on-road autonomous driving events.

Prompt 5#

重点：

golden dataset · offline eval · online metrics · confidence calibration · model/prompt rollout

28. 一句话总结#

这个岗位最强的 system design stance 是：

Build an AI-assisted, evidence-backed, human-in-the-loop release and triage platform. Use LLMs to summarize, correlate, retrieve, and propose root-cause hypotheses, but keep release gates deterministic, auditable, and safety-aware.

你面试时只要抓住这条主线，就会显得非常 senior。

看了你的 PDF 后，这个 role 其实可以非常强地往你身上贴。你不应该把自己包装成“我会做 LLM app”，而应该包装成：

AI infra / multimodal platform tech lead who can build production-grade LLM-driven release & triage tooling.

你的 resume 里最强的 matching points 是：

  1. Multimodal + LLM workflow 平台经验

你现在的 summary 就是 “AI data infrastructure, multimodal systems, production ML platforms”，并且覆盖 ingestion、indexing、retrieval、model serving、workflow orchestration、resource optimization。这个和岗位里的 logs / metrics / images / video / LLM triage 高度重合。

  1. 技术栈非常 match

你的 skills 里有 Spark、Kafka、Flink、Iceberg、Kubernetes、Airflow、Argo、Kubeflow、Redis、Milvus、Elasticsearch、Prometheus、Grafana、OpenTelemetry、Ray Serve、vLLM、Feast 等等。这个可以直接支撑 release data pipeline、artifact processing、retrieval、observability、model serving。

  1. adt AI Infra 经历非常像这个岗位的核心工作

你写了 context-aware AI infrastructure platform，支持 multimodal and LLM-powered workflows、media onboarding、retrieval、generation、semantic tagging、assistant-style workloads，并且 built unified intent registry / execution planner / rollout controls。这个几乎可以平移成 “failure-event intent registry + RCA execution planner + release rollout controls”。

  1. 你有 heavy media processing / async workflow 经验

hybrid edge-cloud execution、edge-first preprocessing、artifact references、checkpointed execution、long-running image/video processing jobs，这个非常适合解释为什么 triage 系统不能把 raw video/logs 全丢给 LLM，而要先做 artifact processing。

  1. 你有大规模 retrieval / vector + lexical search 经验

Milvus + Elasticsearch + Flink + Kafka，50B+ assets，image/audio/video/3D/document modalities，这个可以直接对应 similar incident retrieval、failure clustering、RAG、multimodal evidence search。

你的定位不要说成 release engineer#

这个岗位虽然在 Release & Triage team，但你的定位应该是：

I am not a traditional release engineer. I am a platform / AI infra engineer who can build the release intelligence layer.

中文解释就是：

你不是去手动跑 release checklist 的人。你是去搭一个系统，把 release / simulation / on-road / CI/CD 里的复杂 signals 变成 structured, searchable, explainable, confidence-scored engineering insights 的人。

这比单纯 release engineering 更 senior。

1. 你的 60 秒自我介绍#

可以这样准备：

I’m a senior software engineer and technical lead focused on AI infrastructure, multimodal systems, and production ML platforms.

Most recently at adt, I designed and built a context-aware AI infrastructure platform for multimodal and LLM-powered workflows, including media onboarding, retrieval, semantic tagging, generation, and assistant-style workloads. The platform included an intent registry, execution planner, artifact references, policy checks, model dependency management, runtime selection, and rollout controls.

Before that, I worked on large-scale multimodal discovery systems across Photoshop, Lightroom, and Premiere, using vector and lexical retrieval, streaming pipelines, embedding generation, reranking, and multi-cloud Kubernetes infrastructure to onboard tens of billions of assets across image, video, audio, 3D, and document modalities.

What interests me about this wolf role is that it applies the same platform and AI-infra thinking to a very high-impact release and triage workflow. Instead of using LLMs as a generic chatbot, I would design them as part of an evidence-backed, human-in-the-loop system that helps engineers understand simulation failures, on-road events, CI/CD regressions, and release risk more quickly and safely.

这个版本的关键词非常对：

  • AI infrastructure
  • multimodal systems
  • LLM-powered workflows
  • artifact references
  • execution planner
  • retrieval
  • evidence-backed
  • human-in-the-loop
  • release risk

7. 你面试里的 strongest design stance#

你要反复讲这句话：

LLM should assist release triage, not own release authority.

中文展开：

在 AD/ADAS 或 safety-critical system 里，LLM 可以做：

summarize · retrieve · correlate · propose hypothesis · rank likely owner · explain evidence · generate report

但 LLM 不应该单独决定：

release pass/fail · safety waiver · bug severity downgrade · root cause final truth

最终要靠：

deterministic gate policy · metrics / simulation coverage · historical comparison · human triager approval · audit trail

这会显得你非常 senior。

8. 针对你的版本：system design answer 开头#

你可以这样开场：

I would design this as an AI-assisted release intelligence platform.

The goal is not to build a chatbot that engineers ask questions manually. The goal is to build a workflow-integrated system that automatically ingests CI/CD failures, simulation results, on-road events, logs, metrics, images, and videos; normalizes them into structured failure events; retrieves similar historical incidents; generates evidence-backed root-cause hypotheses; assigns confidence scores; and surfaces release risk to engineers through dashboards and triage workflows.

Given this is a safety-critical domain, I would keep the release gate deterministic and auditable. The LLM can help explain and prioritize failures, but final release decisions should be based on policy, metrics, coverage, severity, and human review.

这个开头非常适合你。

Kafka / Flink:
- ingest CI/CD, simulation, and on-road event streams
- normalize FailureEvent
- aggregate trends

Iceberg / Parquet:
- store simulation/test/log metadata for offline analytics

Object Storage:
- store raw logs, images, videos, traces

Elasticsearch / OpenSearch:
- keyword search over logs, tickets, runbooks, stack traces

Milvus / Vector DB:
- semantic retrieval for similar failures and multimodal embeddings

Ray Serve / vLLM:
- serve LLM / embedding / reranking / vision models

Redis:
- cache hot dashboard metrics
- cache repeated RCA result
- rate limit LLM usage
- store active triage queue state

Prometheus / Grafana / OpenTelemetry:
- observe ingestion, pipeline latency, LLM latency, RCA quality, queue depth

这样你的技术栈不是摆设，而是每个都有 role relevance。

Common data model#

python
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional

class SourceType(str, Enum):
    CI = "CI"
    SIMULATION = "SIMULATION"
    ON_ROAD = "ON_ROAD"

class Severity(str, Enum):
    S0 = "S0"  # safety critical
    S1 = "S1"
    S2 = "S2"
    S3 = "S3"

class EventStatus(str, Enum):
    NEW = "NEW"
    PROCESSING = "PROCESSING"
    TRIAGED = "TRIAGED"
    KNOWN_ISSUE = "KNOWN_ISSUE"
    FIXED = "FIXED"
    WAIVED = "WAIVED"

@dataclass
class ArtifactRefs:
    logs: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    metrics: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    traces: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    images: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    videos: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    code_diffs: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)

@dataclass
class FailureEvent:
    event_id: str
    source_type: SourceType
    release_id: Optional[str]
    build_id: Optional[str]
    commit_sha: Optional[str]
    branch: Optional[str]
    test_suite: Optional[str]
    test_case_id: Optional[str]
    scenario_id: Optional[str]
    vehicle_id: Optional[str]
    software_version: Optional[str]
    timestamp_ms: int
    severity: Severity
    failure_signature: str
    artifact_refs: ArtifactRefs
    metadata: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    status: EventStatus = EventStatus.NEW

@dataclass
class EvidenceRef:
    kind: str  # log, metric, video, image, commit, jira, scenario
    uri: str
    start_ts_ms: Optional[int] = None
    end_ts_ms: Optional[int] = None
    line_start: Optional[int] = None
    line_end: Optional[int] = None
    description: str = ""

@dataclass
class RootCauseHypothesis:
    component: str
    hypothesis: str
    confidence: float
    evidence_refs: List[EvidenceRef]
    counter_evidence: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)

@dataclass
class RCAResult:
    event_id: str
    summary: str
    hypotheses: List[RootCauseHypothesis]
    similar_incidents: List[str]
    regression_likelihood: float
    release_risk_level: str
    recommended_actions: List[str]
    model_version: str
    prompt_version: str
    created_at_ms: int

1. Design an AI-powered triage system for failed autonomous driving simulation tests#

1.1 Problem statement#

设计一个系统，自动处理 autonomous driving simulation test failures。输入是大规模 simulation platform 产生的 test result、scenario metadata、logs、metrics、planner/perception signals、image/video artifacts。系统要自动：

cluster similar failures · detect regressions · retrieve similar historical failures · generate RCA hypotheses · assign likely owner · produce confidence score · push results to triage dashboard / Jira / Slack

这个系统的目标不是替代 triager，而是把 triager 从海量 simulation failures 里解放出来。

1.2 Requirements#

Functional requirements#

  1. Ingest simulation test results at scale.
  2. Normalize every failed test into SimulationFailureEvent.
  3. Group similar failures into clusters.
  4. Compare current release candidate with baseline release.
  5. Retrieve similar historical incidents.
  6. Generate AI-assisted RCA summary.
  7. Predict likely component and owner.
  8. Provide confidence score and supporting evidence.
  9. Support human feedback and correction.
  10. Export triage results to dashboard / Jira / Slack.

Non-functional requirements#

  1. Scale: millions of simulation scenarios per day.
  2. Latency:

cluster-level triage: within 5–15 minutes after batch finishes · single critical failure: near real-time, within 1–3 minutes

  1. Reliability: simulation pipeline cannot be blocked by LLM failures.
  2. Cost control: LLM analyzes representative clusters, not every failure.
  3. Auditability: every RCA must link to evidence.
  4. Safety: high-severity failures require human approval.

1.3 BOTE estimation#

假设：

text
Simulation scenarios per day: 5M
Failure rate: 1% = 50K failed scenarios/day
Average log per failure: 10 MB compressed
Average metric summary: 100 KB
Average video clip: 50 MB
Interesting high-severity failures needing video analysis: 5%

Storage:

text
Logs: 50K * 10 MB = 500 GB/day
Metrics: 50K * 100 KB = 5 GB/day
Video subset: 2.5K * 50 MB = 125 GB/day
Embedding chunks:
  50K failures * 20 chunks * 1536 dim * 4 bytes ≈ 6 GB/day raw vector data

Conclusion:

raw artifacts → Object Storage / Data Lake · structured metadata → Postgres / Spanner · analytical results → Iceberg / BigQuery / Snowflake · vector / semantic search → Milvus / pgvector / OpenSearch vector · hot dashboard / active queue → Redis

1.5 Simulation-specific data model#

python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Dict, List, Optional

@dataclass
class SimulationScenario:
    scenario_id: str
    scenario_type: str          # lane_change, unprotected_left, cut_in, pedestrian_crossing
    map_region: str
    odd: Dict[str, str]         # weather, lighting, road_type, traffic_density
    seed: Optional[int]
    expected_behavior: str

@dataclass
class SimulationFailureFeatures:
    event_id: str
    scenario_id: str
    release_id: str
    failure_signature: str
    component_hint: str
    planner_latency_p95_ms: float
    perception_confidence_min: float
    trajectory_deviation_m: float
    min_ttc_sec: float
    collision: bool
    disengagement: bool
    log_embedding: List[float]
    scenario_embedding: List[float]
    video_embedding: Optional[List[float]]

1.6 Failure signature design#

核心是不要直接用 raw log。要 normalize：

text
failure_signature =
  scenario_type
+ violated_safety_metric
+ normalized_error_log_template
+ component_hint
+ metric_anomaly_pattern
+ software_version_delta

Example:

text
"unprotected_left|min_ttc_below_threshold|planner_cost_spike|planner|new_in_rc3"

1.7 Failure clustering#

Cluster by mixed signals#

text
Clustering features:
- scenario_type
- ODD metadata
- failure_signature
- normalized logs
- metric anomaly features
- trajectory deviation pattern
- safety metric violation
- image/video embedding

Why clustering before LLM?#

因为 50K failures/day 不能每个都 call LLM。应该：

text
50K failures
  → 1K coarse signatures
  → 100 meaningful clusters
  → LLM analyzes 1-3 representative failures per cluster

1.8 PyTorch code: multimodal failure embedding model#

下面是一个面试可讲的 prototype。生产环境里 text encoder 可以换成 internal embedding model / BERT / LLM embedding；video embedding 可以来自 vision model；这里展示 fusion 思路。

python
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

class SimulationFailureFusionModel(nn.Module):
    """
    Fuse structured simulation metrics + log embedding + scenario embedding + optional video embedding.

Production:
    - log_embedding: from internal text embedding model
    - scenario_embedding: from scenario description encoder
    - video_embedding: from vision/video model
    - numeric_features: safety metrics, latency, TTC, deviation, etc.
    """

def __init__(
        self,
        log_dim: int = 768,
        scenario_dim: int = 256,
        video_dim: int = 512,
        numeric_dim: int = 16,
        hidden_dim: int = 256,
        output_dim: int = 128,
        num_components: int = 20,
    ):
        super().__init__()

self.log_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(log_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
        )

self.scenario_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(scenario_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
        )

self.video_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(video_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
        )

self.numeric_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(numeric_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
        )

self.fusion = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim * 4, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.1),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, output_dim),
        )

self.component_head = nn.Linear(output_dim, num_components)
        self.severity_head = nn.Linear(output_dim, 4)
        self.regression_head = nn.Linear(output_dim, 1)

def forward(
        self,
        log_emb: torch.Tensor,
        scenario_emb: torch.Tensor,
        video_emb: torch.Tensor,
        numeric_features: torch.Tensor,
        video_mask: torch.Tensor,
    ):
        """
        video_mask: shape [B, 1], 1 if video available, 0 if missing.
        """

log_h = self.log_proj(log_emb)
        scenario_h = self.scenario_proj(scenario_emb)
        video_h = self.video_proj(video_emb) * video_mask
        numeric_h = self.numeric_proj(numeric_features)

fused = torch.cat([log_h, scenario_h, video_h, numeric_h], dim=-1)
        z = self.fusion(fused)
        z = F.normalize(z, p=2, dim=-1)

return {
            "embedding": z,
            "component_logits": self.component_head(z),
            "severity_logits": self.severity_head(z),
            "regression_logit": self.regression_head(z).squeeze(-1),
        }

def multi_task_loss(outputs, labels):
    component_loss = F.cross_entropy(outputs["component_logits"], labels["component"])
    severity_loss = F.cross_entropy(outputs["severity_logits"], labels["severity"])
    regression_loss = F.binary_cross_entropy_with_logits(
        outputs["regression_logit"],
        labels["is_regression"].float(),
    )

return component_loss + 0.5 * severity_loss + 0.7 * regression_loss

1.9 Python code: clustering failures#

python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Dict, List
import numpy as np
from sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler

@dataclass
class ClusteredFailure:
    event_id: str
    cluster_id: int
    distance_to_centroid: float

class FailureClusterer:
    def __init__(self, eps: float = 0.35, min_samples: int = 5):
        self.eps = eps
        self.min_samples = min_samples

def cluster(self, event_ids: List[str], embeddings: np.ndarray) -> List[ClusteredFailure]:
        """
        embeddings: [N, D], already normalized.
        """
        if len(event_ids) != len(embeddings):
            raise ValueError("event_ids and embeddings size mismatch")

clustering = DBSCAN(
            eps=self.eps,
            min_samples=self.min_samples,
            metric="cosine",
        )
        labels = clustering.fit_predict(embeddings)

centroids: Dict[int, np.ndarray] = {}
        for label in set(labels):
            if label == -1:
                continue
            cluster_vecs = embeddings[labels == label]
            centroids[label] = cluster_vecs.mean(axis=0)

results: List[ClusteredFailure] = []
        for event_id, label, emb in zip(event_ids, labels, embeddings):
            if label == -1:
                dist = 1.0
            else:
                centroid = centroids[label]
                dist = float(1.0 - np.dot(emb, centroid) / (
                    np.linalg.norm(emb) * np.linalg.norm(centroid) + 1e-8
                ))
            results.append(ClusteredFailure(event_id, int(label), dist))

return results

1.10 AI Triage Orchestrator#

python
from typing import Protocol, List, Dict, Any
import time

class Retriever(Protocol):
    def retrieve_similar_failures(self, event: FailureEvent, top_k: int) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        ...

class LLMClient(Protocol):
    def generate_json(self, prompt: str, schema: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        ...

class ConfidenceScorer(Protocol):
    def score(self, event: FailureEvent, llm_output: Dict[str, Any], retrieved: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> float:
        ...

class SimulationTriageOrchestrator:
    def __init__(self, retriever: Retriever, llm: LLMClient, scorer: ConfidenceScorer):
        self.retriever = retriever
        self.llm = llm
        self.scorer = scorer

def triage_cluster(self, representative_event: FailureEvent) -> RCAResult:
        similar = self.retriever.retrieve_similar_failures(representative_event, top_k=8)

prompt = self._build_prompt(representative_event, similar)

schema = {
            "summary": "string",
            "hypotheses": "list",
            "regression_likelihood": "float",
            "release_risk_level": "string",
            "recommended_actions": "list",
        }

llm_output = self.llm.generate_json(prompt, schema=schema)
        confidence = self.scorer.score(representative_event, llm_output, similar)

hypotheses = []
        for h in llm_output.get("hypotheses", []):
            hypotheses.append(
                RootCauseHypothesis(
                    component=h["component"],
                    hypothesis=h["hypothesis"],
                    confidence=min(float(h.get("confidence", confidence)), confidence),
                    evidence_refs=[
                        EvidenceRef(
                            kind=e["kind"],
                            uri=e["uri"],
                            description=e.get("description", ""),
                        )
                        for e in h.get("evidence_refs", [])
                    ],
                    counter_evidence=h.get("counter_evidence", []),
                )
            )

return RCAResult(
            event_id=representative_event.event_id,
            summary=llm_output["summary"],
            hypotheses=hypotheses,
            similar_incidents=[s["incident_id"] for s in similar if "incident_id" in s],
            regression_likelihood=float(llm_output.get("regression_likelihood", 0.0)),
            release_risk_level=llm_output.get("release_risk_level", "NEED_REVIEW"),
            recommended_actions=llm_output.get("recommended_actions", []),
            model_version="llm-prod-2026-07",
            prompt_version="sim-triage-v3",
            created_at_ms=int(time.time() * 1000),
        )

def _build_prompt(self, event: FailureEvent, similar: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> str:
        return f"""
You are an autonomous driving simulation triage assistant.

Task:
- Summarize the simulation failure.
- Identify likely root-cause hypotheses.
- Cite evidence only from provided logs, metrics, scenario metadata, or similar incidents.
- If evidence is insufficient, say unknown.
- Do not make unsupported claims.
- Separate hypothesis from confirmed root cause.

Failure event:
{event}

Similar historical incidents:
{similar}

Return JSON only.
"""

1.11 Deep dive: regression detection#

python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Optional

@dataclass
class ReleaseComparison:
    current_fail_rate: float
    baseline_fail_rate: float
    current_count: int
    baseline_count: int
    first_bad_commit: Optional[str]
    last_good_commit: Optional[str]

class RegressionDetector:
    def __init__(self, min_delta: float = 0.02, min_count: int = 5):
        self.min_delta = min_delta
        self.min_count = min_count

def score_regression(self, cmp: ReleaseComparison) -> float:
        """
        Simple interpretable regression score.
        Production can use Bayesian test / sequential testing / causal commit correlation.
        """
        delta = cmp.current_fail_rate - cmp.baseline_fail_rate

if cmp.current_count < self.min_count:
            return 0.2

score = 0.0

if delta > self.min_delta:
            score += min(delta / 0.2, 1.0) * 0.5

if cmp.baseline_fail_rate == 0 and cmp.current_fail_rate > 0:
            score += 0.3

if cmp.first_bad_commit and cmp.last_good_commit:
            score += 0.2

return min(score, 1.0)

1.12 Tradeoffs#

ChoiceProsCons
Cluster first, LLM secondcost low, scalablerare unique failures may be delayed
Analyze every failurehigh recallimpossible cost, noisy
Deterministic rules firststable, cheapless flexible
LLM firstflexible summaryhallucination/cost risk
Vector search onlysemantic matchmay miss exact error
Hybrid searchbetter precisionmore infra complexity

1.13 Failure modes#

  1. LLM gives unsupported RCA

Mitigation: evidence verifier, schema validation, confidence penalty.

  1. Clustering merges unrelated failures

Mitigation: metadata constraints, cluster purity check, human split.

  1. Rare safety-critical issue hidden in large cluster

Mitigation: severity-first priority, outlier detection.

  1. Simulation environment outage creates false regression

Mitigation: infra health signal, environment version included in signature.

  1. Missing artifacts

Mitigation: data completeness score and low-confidence label.

2. Design a release qualification dashboard for AD/ADAS software#

2.1 Problem statement#

设计一个 Release Qualification Dashboard，帮助 release engineers、triagers、AD developers 决定一个 AD/ADAS release candidate 是否 ready。

Dashboard 要汇总：

  • CI/CD status
  • simulation coverage
  • simulation pass/fail trend
  • on-road incident signals
  • known issues
  • new regressions
  • blocker bugs
  • flaky tests
  • risk score
  • release gate decision

核心：它不是普通 dashboard，而是 release decision support system。

2.2 Requirements#

Functional requirements#

  1. Show release candidate health.
  2. Compare current release with previous baseline.
  3. Track simulation test coverage and failure clusters.
  4. Track CI/CD failures and deployment readiness.
  5. Track open blockers and waived issues.
  6. Surface new regressions and severity.
  7. Compute release risk score.
  8. Support deterministic gate policy.
  9. Provide drill-down to evidence and RCA.
  10. Generate daily / per-RC release report.

Non-functional requirements#

  1. Low dashboard latency: seconds for cached view.
  2. Freshness: within 1–5 minutes from new signal.
  3. High availability for release process.
  4. Auditability: historical gate decision should be reproducible.
  5. Role-based access control.
  6. Explainable risk score.

2.3 BOTE#

假设：

text
Release candidates per week: 5
Test suites per RC: 500
Simulation scenarios per RC: 10M
Failure clusters per RC: 100-500
Open blocker bugs: 50-200
Dashboard users: 100-1000 internal users

Dashboard query pattern:

many repeated reads · few writes from pipelines · drill-down queries can be heavy · top-level page must be cached/materialized

Conclusion:

top-level release summary → materialized view + Redis cache · raw analytics → OLAP table / Iceberg / BigQuery · drill-down → query service · gate decision snapshot → immutable audit record

text
                  ┌────────────────────────────┐
                  │ Release Candidate Registry │

2.6 Gate policy design#

最重要的原则：

Release gate 是 deterministic policy，不是 LLM free text。

Example policy:

text
BLOCK if:
- critical_safety_violations > 0
- simulation_coverage < required threshold
- any S0/S1 new regression cluster is untriaged
- required CI suite failed
- open blocker has no owner or no waiver

NEED_REVIEW if:
- new S2 regression clusters exceed threshold
- LLM confidence is low for high-impact failures
- flaky test rate increased sharply
- coverage gap exists in important ODD

WARN if:
- non-critical regressions exist but triaged
- known issues have valid waivers
- low confidence but low severity

PASS if:
- deterministic gates pass
- no untriaged high-severity regression
- coverage sufficient
- risk score under threshold

2.7 Risk score formula#

python
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass
class ReleaseSignals:
    ci_pass_rate: float
    simulation_pass_rate: float
    simulation_coverage: float
    critical_safety_violations: int
    new_s1_regressions: int
    new_s2_regressions: int
    open_blockers: int
    flaky_test_rate: float
    avg_rca_confidence: float

class ReleaseRiskScorer:
    """
    Interpretable release risk score.
    0 = very low risk, 100 = highest risk.
    """

def score(self, s: ReleaseSignals) -> tuple[float, list[str]]:
        score = 0.0
        reasons: list[str] = []

if s.critical_safety_violations > 0:
            score += 40
            reasons.append(f"{s.critical_safety_violations} critical safety violations")

if s.simulation_coverage < 0.95:
            penalty = (0.95 - s.simulation_coverage) * 100
            score += min(20, penalty)
            reasons.append(f"Simulation coverage below threshold: {s.simulation_coverage:.2%}")

if s.ci_pass_rate < 0.98:
            score += min(15, (0.98 - s.ci_pass_rate) * 100)
            reasons.append(f"CI pass rate below target: {s.ci_pass_rate:.2%}")

if s.new_s1_regressions > 0:
            score += 20 * s.new_s1_regressions
            reasons.append(f"{s.new_s1_regressions} new S1 regressions")

if s.new_s2_regressions > 0:
            score += min(15, 3 * s.new_s2_regressions)
            reasons.append(f"{s.new_s2_regressions} new S2 regressions")

if s.open_blockers > 0:
            score += min(20, 2 * s.open_blockers)
            reasons.append(f"{s.open_blockers} open blockers")

if s.flaky_test_rate > 0.03:
            score += min(10, s.flaky_test_rate * 100)
            reasons.append(f"Flaky test rate high: {s.flaky_test_rate:.2%}")

if s.avg_rca_confidence < 0.6:
            score += 10
            reasons.append(f"Low average RCA confidence: {s.avg_rca_confidence:.2f}")

return min(score, 100.0), reasons

class GateDecisionEngine:
    def decide(self, signals: ReleaseSignals) -> tuple[str, list[str]]:
        risk_score, reasons = ReleaseRiskScorer().score(signals)

if signals.critical_safety_violations > 0:
            return "BLOCK", reasons

if signals.simulation_coverage < 0.90:
            return "BLOCK", reasons

if signals.new_s1_regressions > 0:
            return "BLOCK", reasons

if risk_score >= 60:
            return "NEED_REVIEW", reasons

if risk_score >= 30:
            return "WARN", reasons

return "PASS", reasons

2.8 Dashboard API#

python
from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException
from typing import Dict

app = FastAPI(title="Release Qualification Dashboard API")

---

## In production these are DB/cache clients.
release_snapshots: Dict[str, ReleaseReadinessSnapshot] = {}

@app.get("/v1/releases/{release_id}/readiness")
def get_release_readiness(release_id: str):
    snapshot = release_snapshots.get(release_id)
    if not snapshot:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Release not found")
    return snapshot

@app.get("/v1/releases/{release_id}/failure-clusters")
def get_failure_clusters(release_id: str, severity: str | None = None):
    snapshot = release_snapshots.get(release_id)
    if not snapshot:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Release not found")

clusters = snapshot.top_failure_clusters
    if severity:
        clusters = [c for c in clusters if c.severity == severity]
    return {"clusters": clusters}

@app.post("/v1/releases/{release_id}/gate/evaluate")
def evaluate_gate(release_id: str, signals: ReleaseSignals):
    decision, reasons = GateDecisionEngine().decide(signals)
    risk_score, _ = ReleaseRiskScorer().score(signals)

# Production:
    # - persist immutable gate snapshot
    # - write audit log
    # - notify release channel if BLOCK or NEED_REVIEW
    return {
        "release_id": release_id,
        "gate_decision": decision,
        "risk_score": risk_score,
        "reasons": reasons,
    }

2.10 Materialized view update flow#

text
1. New CI/simulation/triage signal arrives.
2. Aggregation worker updates OLAP tables.
3. Release Risk Service recomputes snapshot.
4. Snapshot stored in metadata DB.
5. Hot summary cached in Redis.
6. Dashboard reads Redis first, falls back to DB.
7. Every gate decision creates immutable audit snapshot.

2.11 PyTorch code: risk calibration model#

Interpretable rules are necessary, but we can add an ML model to calibrate risk based on historical releases. It should assist, not replace deterministic gate.

python
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

class ReleaseRiskCalibrationModel(nn.Module):
    """
    Predict probability that a release candidate will cause post-release incident
    or require rollback / hotfix.

This should NOT replace deterministic safety gates.
    It helps calibrate risk score and rank review priority.
    """

def __init__(self, input_dim: int = 32, hidden_dim: int = 64):
        super().__init__()
        self.net = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.1),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1),
        )

def forward(self, features: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.net(features).squeeze(-1)

def train_risk_model(model, dataloader, optimizer, device):
    model.train()
    total_loss = 0.0

for batch in dataloader:
        x = batch["features"].to(device)
        y = batch["post_release_incident"].float().to(device)

logits = model(x)
        loss = F.binary_cross_entropy_with_logits(logits, y)

optimizer.zero_grad()
        loss.backward()
        optimizer.step()

total_loss += loss.item() * x.size(0)

return total_loss / len(dataloader.dataset)

@torch.no_grad()
def predict_risk_probability(model, features, device):
    model.eval()
    x = torch.tensor(features, dtype=torch.float32, device=device).unsqueeze(0)
    prob = torch.sigmoid(model(x)).item()
    return prob

2.12 Tradeoffs#

Design choiceProsCons
Rule-based gateauditable, deterministicmay miss subtle risk
ML-calibrated risklearns historical patternsneeds careful validation
Redis materialized viewfast UIstale risk if update bug
Query raw OLAP liveaccurateslow for dashboard
Human waiver supportpracticalmust be audited

2.13 Interview sound bite#

For release qualification, I would separate three layers: signal aggregation, risk interpretation, and gate decision. LLM can help explain failures, but the gate itself should be deterministic, auditable, and reproducible.

3. Design an LLM-based root cause analysis assistant for CI/CD failures#

3.1 Problem statement#

设计一个 LLM-based assistant，帮助 developers / release engineers 分析 CI/CD failures：

输入：

  • build logs
  • test logs
  • stack traces
  • commit diff
  • changed files
  • code ownership
  • previous failures
  • flaky test history
  • deployment config
  • test environment metadata

输出：

concise failure summary · suspected root cause · likely owner · similar past failure · flaky vs regression classification · recommended next action · confidence and evidence

3.2 Requirements#

Functional#

  1. Ingest build/test failure events from CI/CD.
  2. Extract useful logs and stack traces.
  3. Retrieve similar historical failures.
  4. Analyze commit diff and ownership.
  5. Classify failure:

code regression · flaky test · infra failure · dependency/config issue · test data issue

  1. Generate RCA report.
  2. Post back to CI UI / PR / Slack / Jira.
  3. Collect developer feedback.

Non-functional#

  1. Low latency: summary within 1–3 minutes.
  2. CI should not block on LLM.
  3. Support many parallel builds.
  4. Avoid leaking secrets from logs.
  5. Must cite evidence.
  6. Must allow “unknown.”

3.3 Architecture#

text
                    ┌─────────────────────────────┐
                    │          CI/CD System         │
                    │ Buildkite / Harness / GitHub │
                    └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                                   │ webhook/event
                                   ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    CI Failure Ingestion Service                   │
│ build_id, job_id, test_id, commit_sha, artifact refs              │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────┘
               │                              │
               ▼                              ▼
┌─────────────────────────────┐    ┌───────────────────────────────┐
│ Build Metadata Store         │    │ CI Artifact Store              │
│ commits, owners, status      │    │ logs, junit xml, traces         │
└──────────────┬──────────────┘    └───────────────┬───────────────┘
               │                                   │
               ▼                                   ▼

│                      Log & Diff Processor                         │

               ▼                              ▼

3.5 Log processing#

Important design#

不要把完整 log 丢给 LLM。先做：

text
Raw log
  → redact secrets
  → split by test/job section
  → extract error windows
  → extract stack traces
  → normalize paths/timestamps
  → generate compact context

3.6 Python code: log extraction and redaction#

python
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import List

SECRET_PATTERNS = [
    re.compile(r"(?i)(api[_-]?key|token|secret|password)\s*[:=]\s*['\"]?([A-Za-z0-9_\-\.=]+)"),
    re.compile(r"AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}"),  # AWS-like access key pattern
]

@dataclass
class LogWindow:
    start_line: int
    end_line: int
    text: str

class LogProcessor:
    ERROR_PATTERNS = [
        re.compile(r"error", re.IGNORECASE),
        re.compile(r"exception", re.IGNORECASE),
        re.compile(r"failed", re.IGNORECASE),
        re.compile(r"traceback", re.IGNORECASE),
        re.compile(r"assert", re.IGNORECASE),
    ]

def redact(self, text: str) -> str:
        redacted = text
        for pattern in SECRET_PATTERNS:
            redacted = pattern.sub(lambda m: m.group(0).replace(m.group(2), "[REDACTED]") if len(m.groups()) >= 2 else "[REDACTED]", redacted)
        return redacted

def extract_error_windows(self, log_text: str, context_lines: int = 30) -> List[LogWindow]:
        lines = log_text.splitlines()
        error_indices = []

for i, line in enumerate(lines):
            if any(p.search(line) for p in self.ERROR_PATTERNS):
                error_indices.append(i)

windows = []
        seen = set()

for idx in error_indices:
            start = max(0, idx - context_lines)
            end = min(len(lines), idx + context_lines + 1)
            key = (start, end)
            if key in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(key)

text = "\n".join(lines[start:end])
            windows.append(LogWindow(start_line=start + 1, end_line=end, text=self.redact(text)))

return windows[:10]

def normalize_signature(self, text: str) -> str:
        text = re.sub(r"0x[0-9a-fA-F]+", "0xADDR", text)
        text = re.sub(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T[\d:\.Z\-]+", "TIMESTAMP", text)
        text = re.sub(r"\b\d+\b", "NUM", text)
        text = re.sub(r"/[A-Za-z0-9_\-/\.]+", "/PATH", text)
        return text[:2000]

3.7 PyTorch code: CI failure classifier#

python
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

class CIFailureClassifier(nn.Module):
    """
    Classify CI failures into:
    0 code_regression
    1 flaky_test
    2 infra_failure
    3 config_dependency
    4 test_data
    5 unknown
    """

def __init__(self, text_emb_dim: int = 768, diff_emb_dim: int = 768, meta_dim: int = 20, hidden: int = 256):
        super().__init__()

self.text_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(text_emb_dim, hidden),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden),
        )

self.diff_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(diff_emb_dim, hidden),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden),
        )

self.meta_proj = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(meta_dim, hidden),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden),
        )

self.classifier = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(hidden * 3, hidden),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.1),
            nn.Linear(hidden, 6),
        )

self.owner_head = nn.Linear(hidden * 3, 100)  # example: 100 owner teams

def forward(self, log_emb, diff_emb, meta_features):
        h1 = self.text_proj(log_emb)
        h2 = self.diff_proj(diff_emb)
        h3 = self.meta_proj(meta_features)

h = torch.cat([h1, h2, h3], dim=-1)
        category_logits = self.classifier(h)
        owner_logits = self.owner_head(h)

return {
            "category_logits": category_logits,
            "owner_logits": owner_logits,
        }

def ci_classifier_loss(outputs, labels):
    category_loss = F.cross_entropy(outputs["category_logits"], labels["category"])
    owner_loss = F.cross_entropy(outputs["owner_logits"], labels["owner"])
    return category_loss + 0.5 * owner_loss

3.8 LLM prompt structure#

面试时讲 prompt 不是随便写，而是 structured context：

text
System:
You are a CI/CD failure triage assistant.
You must only use provided evidence.
If uncertain, say unknown.
Return JSON.

Context:
- build metadata
- commit diff summary
- changed files and owners
- extracted log windows
- stack traces
- similar historical failures
- flaky history
- infrastructure health

Tasks:
1. summarize failure
2. classify failure
3. identify likely owner
4. list evidence
5. list counter-evidence
6. recommend next action

3.9 Python code: CI RCA assistant#

python
from typing import Any, Dict, List

class CIRCAAssistant:
    def __init__(self, log_processor: LogProcessor, retriever: Retriever, llm: LLMClient):
        self.log_processor = log_processor
        self.retriever = retriever
        self.llm = llm

def analyze(self, event: CIFailureEvent, raw_log: str, diff_summary: str) -> CIFailureAnalysis:
        windows = self.log_processor.extract_error_windows(raw_log)
        signature_context = "\n\n".join(w.text for w in windows[:3])

# Convert CIFailureEvent to shared FailureEvent if using common retriever.
        shared_event = FailureEvent(
            event_id=f"{event.build_id}:{event.job_id}",
            source_type=SourceType.CI,
            release_id=None,
            build_id=event.build_id,
            commit_sha=event.commit_sha,
            branch=event.branch,
            test_suite=None,
            test_case_id=event.test_name,
            scenario_id=None,
            vehicle_id=None,
            software_version=None,
            timestamp_ms=0,
            severity=Severity.S3,
            failure_signature=event.failure_signature,
            artifact_refs=ArtifactRefs(logs=[event.log_uri], code_diffs=[event.diff_uri] if event.diff_uri else []),
        )

similar = self.retriever.retrieve_similar_failures(shared_event, top_k=5)

prompt = self._build_prompt(event, signature_context, diff_summary, similar)

output = self.llm.generate_json(
            prompt,
            schema={
                "summary": "string",
                "category": "string",
                "suspected_owner": "string",
                "suspected_files": "list",
                "similar_failures": "list",
                "confidence": "float",
                "evidence": "list",
                "recommended_actions": "list",
            },
        )

return CIFailureAnalysis(
            summary=output["summary"],
            category=output.get("category", "unknown"),
            suspected_owner=output.get("suspected_owner"),
            suspected_files=output.get("suspected_files", []),
            similar_failures=output.get("similar_failures", []),
            confidence=float(output.get("confidence", 0.0)),
            evidence=[
                EvidenceRef(
                    kind=e.get("kind", "log"),
                    uri=e.get("uri", event.log_uri),
                    line_start=e.get("line_start"),
                    line_end=e.get("line_end"),
                    description=e.get("description", ""),
                )
                for e in output.get("evidence", [])
            ],
            recommended_actions=output.get("recommended_actions", []),
        )

def _build_prompt(
        self,
        event: CIFailureEvent,
        log_context: str,
        diff_summary: str,
        similar: List[Dict[str, Any]],
    ) -> str:
        return f"""
You are a CI/CD root-cause analysis assistant.

Rules:
- Use only the provided evidence.
- Do not invent files, owners, or root causes.
- If evidence is weak, return category = "unknown" and lower confidence.
- Distinguish confirmed evidence from hypothesis.
- Return JSON only.

CI failure event:
{event}

Relevant log windows:
{log_context}

Commit diff summary:
{diff_summary}

Similar historical failures:
{similar}

Return:
summary, category, suspected_owner, suspected_files, similar_failures,
confidence, evidence, recommended_actions.
"""

3.10 CI comment example#

text
AI RCA Summary

Category: likely code regression
Confidence: 0.72
Suspected owner: planner-runtime

Summary:
The failing test appears to be caused by a timeout in the planner runtime initialization path.
The same test passed on the previous build and started failing after commit abc123.

Evidence:
- Log lines 421-463 show PlannerRuntimeInitTimeout.
- Commit abc123 changed planner runtime config loading.
- Similar failure INC-8842 had the same stack trace.

Counter-evidence:
- Infra health checks were normal.
- No broad test-suite timeout spike detected.

Recommended action:
- Re-run planner_runtime_init_test with debug config.
- Ask planner-runtime owner to review abc123.

3.11 Failure modes#

  1. Secret leakage from logs

Mitigation: redaction before indexing and before LLM.

  1. LLM blames wrong commit

Mitigation: commit correlation is separate deterministic module.

  1. Flaky test misclassified as regression

Mitigation: flaky history, repeated runs, confidence penalty.

  1. Similar failure retrieval wrong

Mitigation: hybrid search + metadata filters.

  1. Developer distrusts AI comment

Mitigation: concise, evidence-backed, optional, feedback buttons.

3.12 Interview sound bite#

For CI/CD RCA, I would not build a chatbot. I would build a workflow-integrated assistant that comments directly on the failed build or PR, but every statement must be backed by logs, diffs, test history, or similar incidents.

4. Design a multimodal incident review platform for on-road autonomous driving events#

4.1 Problem statement#

设计一个平台，帮助 review on-road AD events。这些事件来自真实道路测试，包含：

  • vehicle logs
  • perception outputs
  • planner decisions
  • localization
  • control signals
  • metrics
  • camera images / videos
  • operator notes
  • map / route context

系统要帮助 triager 快速理解：

what happened? · when did it happen? · what did the vehicle perceive? · what did the planner decide? · was this a known issue? · is it safety critical? · who should investigate? · how does it affect release risk?

4.2 Requirements#

Functional requirements#

  1. Ingest on-road events and artifact references.
  2. Align multimodal signals by timestamp.
  3. Extract relevant event window.
  4. Process videos into keyframes / clips / captions.
  5. Summarize event timeline.
  6. Retrieve similar prior incidents.
  7. Generate RCA hypotheses.
  8. Classify severity and component.
  9. Provide human review UI.
  10. Feed confirmed labels back to release risk and model eval.

Non-functional requirements#

  1. Privacy and security are critical.
  2. Raw video storage is expensive; avoid unnecessary copying.
  3. Latency:

metadata summary: minutes · full video analysis: async

  1. High reliability and auditability.
  2. Must support large artifact sizes.
  3. Must handle missing/corrupted sensor data.

4.3 Architecture#

text
                      ┌──────────────────────────────┐
                      │       On-road Test Fleet       │
                      │ logs / sensor / camera / notes │
                      └───────────────┬──────────────┘
                                      │
                                      ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    On-road Event Ingestion                        │
│ event metadata / artifact refs / vehicle state / route context    │
└──────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┘
               │                               │
               ▼                               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ Event Metadata Store         │     │ Secure Artifact Store          │
│ event, vehicle, version      │     │ raw video, logs, sensor data    │
└──────────────┬──────────────┘     └───────────────┬──────────────┘
               │                                    │
               ▼                                    ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  Multimodal Processing Pipeline                   │
│ timestamp alignment / event window / keyframes / captions         │
│ object tracks / metric anomalies / trajectory comparison           │
└──────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┘
               │                               │
               ▼                               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ Timeline Store               │     │ Multimodal Search Index         │
│ aligned events by timestamp  │     │ text + visual embeddings        │
└──────────────┬──────────────┘     └───────────────┬──────────────┘
               │                                    │
               └──────────────┬─────────────────────┘
                              ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     Incident Review AI Layer                      │
│ timeline summarizer / similar incident retrieval / RCA hypotheses │
│ severity classifier / confidence scorer / evidence verifier       │
└──────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┘
               │                               │
               ▼                               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ Human Review UI              │     │ Release / Triage Feedback       │
│ video timeline + signals     │     │ labels, owners, release risk     │
└─────────────────────────────┘     └──────────────────────────────┘

4.4 On-road event data model#

from dataclasses import dataclass

class TimelineSignal:
    ts_ms: int
    signal_type: str  # perception, planner, control, localization, operator, video
    summary: str

    evidence_ref: EvidenceRef

@dataclass
class IncidentReviewResult:

    event_summary: str
