## 4. PyTorch: reranker and confidence calibration

---

## ============================================================

class EvidenceReranker(nn.Module):
    """
    Rerank retrieved evidence against query/event embedding.

Input:
    - query embedding
    - candidate embedding
    - candidate metadata features
    """

def __init__(self, emb_dim: int = 128, meta_dim: int = 16, hidden_dim: int = 128):
        super().__init__()
        self.net = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(emb_dim * 3 + meta_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.1),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1),
        )

def forward(
        self,
        query_emb: torch.Tensor,
        candidate_emb: torch.Tensor,
        candidate_meta: torch.Tensor,
    ) -> torch.Tensor:
        features = torch.cat(
            [
                query_emb,
                candidate_emb,
                torch.abs(query_emb - candidate_emb),
                candidate_meta,
            ],
            dim=-1,
        )
        return self.net(features).squeeze(-1)

class ConfidenceCalibrator(nn.Module):
    """
    Predict calibrated confidence for an RCA hypothesis.

This is better than trusting LLM self-confidence.

Features can include:
    - number of evidence refs
    - retrieval similarity
    - signal agreement
    - data completeness
    - severity
    - historical acceptance rate
    - model consistency
    """

def __init__(self, feature_dim: int = 12, hidden_dim: int = 64):
        super().__init__()
        self.net = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(feature_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.LayerNorm(hidden_dim),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1),
        )

def forward(self, features: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.net(features).squeeze(-1)

def confidence_features(
    evidence_count: int,
    top_similarity: float,
    has_log: bool,
    has_metric: bool,
    has_video: bool,
    data_completeness: float,
    signal_agreement: float,
    severity: str,
) -> torch.Tensor:
    severity_map = {"S0": 1.0, "S1": 0.8, "S2": 0.5, "S3": 0.2}
    values = [
        min(evidence_count / 5.0, 1.0),
        top_similarity,
        1.0 if has_log else 0.0,
        1.0 if has_metric else 0.0,
        1.0 if has_video else 0.0,
        data_completeness,
        signal_agreement,
        severity_map.get(severity, 0.3),
        0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0,
    ]
    return torch.tensor(values, dtype=torch.float32)

## 5. LanceDB vector index

---

## ============================================================

class LanceArtifactIndex:
    """
    LanceDB-backed vector index.

Production:
    - partition by org/project/release
    - store in object storage
    - add scalar indices on release_id/component/artifact_type
    - use ACL-aware retrieval
    """

def __init__(self, uri: str = "./.lancedb", table_name: str = "artifacts"):
        import lancedb

self.db = lancedb.connect(uri)
        self.table_name = table_name

existing = self.db.table_names()
        if table_name in existing:
            self.table = self.db.open_table(table_name)
        else:
            # Create a minimal table with one dummy row, then delete it.
            data = [
                {
                    "artifact_id": "__init__",
                    "artifact_type": "doc",
                    "uri": "memory://init",
                    "text": "init",
                    "release_id": "",
                    "component": "",
                    "severity": "",
                    "vector": [0.0] * 128,
                    "metadata_json": "{}",
                }
            ]
            self.table = self.db.create_table(table_name, data=data)
            self.table.delete("artifact_id = '__init__'")

def upsert_artifacts(self, artifacts: List[Artifact], embedder: TorchEmbeddingService) -> None:
        rows = []

for a in artifacts:
            vector = embedder.embed_artifact(a)
            rows.append(
                {
                    "artifact_id": a.artifact_id,
                    "artifact_type": a.artifact_type,
                    "uri": a.uri,
                    "text": a.text[:4000],
                    "release_id": str(a.metadata.get("release_id", "")),
                    "component": str(a.metadata.get("component", "")),
                    "severity": str(a.metadata.get("severity", "")),
                    "vector": vector,
                    "metadata_json": json.dumps(a.metadata),
                }
            )

if rows:
            # For demo, append. Production should use merge_insert/upsert by artifact_id.
            self.table.add(rows)

def search(
        self,
        query_vector: List[float],
        limit: int = 5,
        release_id: Optional[str] = None,
        component: Optional[str] = None,
        artifact_type: Optional[str] = None,
    ) -> List[EvidenceRef]:
        query = self.table.search(query_vector).distance_type("cosine").limit(limit)

filters = []
        if release_id:
            filters.append(f"release_id = '{release_id}'")
        if component:
            filters.append(f"component = '{component}'")
        if artifact_type:
            filters.append(f"artifact_type = '{artifact_type}'")

if filters:
            query = query.where(" AND ".join(filters), prefilter=True)

df = query.to_pandas()

results = []
        for _, row in df.iterrows():
            metadata = json.loads(row.get("metadata_json", "{}"))
            distance = float(row.get("_distance", 1.0))
            score = 1.0 - distance

results.append(
                EvidenceRef(
                    artifact_id=row["artifact_id"],
                    uri=row["uri"],
                    artifact_type=row["artifact_type"],
                    score=score,
                    snippet=row["text"][:500],
                    metadata=metadata,
                )
            )
        return results

## 6. Feast feature client

---

## ============================================================

class FeastFeatureClient:
    """
    Optional Feast integration.

For this project:
    - online features can feed risk scoring, reranking, owner prediction
    - example features:
      failure_cluster_stats:historical_fix_time
      component_health:recent_regression_rate
      release_stats:flaky_rate
    """

def __init__(self, repo_path: str = "."):
        try:
            from feast import FeatureStore
            self.store = FeatureStore(repo_path=repo_path)
        except Exception:
            self.store = None

def get_component_features(self, component: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        if self.store is None:
            # Fallback demo values.
            return {
                "component": component,
                "recent_regression_rate": 0.05,
                "historical_fix_time_hours": 12.0,
                "owner_acceptance_rate": 0.80,
            }

# Example only. In real Feast repo, these feature names/entities must exist.
        fv = self.store.get_online_features(
            features=[
                "component_health:recent_regression_rate",
                "component_health:historical_fix_time_hours",
                "component_health:owner_acceptance_rate",
            ],
            entity_rows=[{"component": component}],
        ).to_dict()

return {k: v[0] for k, v in fv.items()}

## 7. Redis hot cache

---

## ============================================================

class HotCache:
    def __init__(self, redis_url: str = "redis://localhost:6379/0"):
        try:
            import redis
            self.client = redis.from_url(redis_url)
        except Exception:
            self.client = None
            self.memory: Dict[str, str] = {}

def get_json(self, key: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
        if self.client:
            raw = self.client.get(key)
            return json.loads(raw) if raw else None
        raw = self.memory.get(key)
        return json.loads(raw) if raw else None

def set_json(self, key: str, value: Dict[str, Any], ttl_sec: int = 3600) -> None:
        raw = json.dumps(value)
        if self.client:
            self.client.setex(key, ttl_sec, raw)
        else:
            self.memory[key] = raw

---

## 8. RCA engine: retrieval + reranking + confidence + report

---

## ============================================================

class RCAEngine:
    def __init__(
        self,
        index: LanceArtifactIndex,
        embedder: TorchEmbeddingService,
        feast_client: Optional[FeastFeatureClient] = None,
        cache: Optional[HotCache] = None,
    ):
        self.index = index
        self.embedder = embedder
        self.feast_client = feast_client or FeastFeatureClient()
        self.cache = cache or HotCache()

self.confidence_model = ConfidenceCalibrator()
        self.confidence_model.eval()

def analyze(self, event: FailureEvent) -> RCAResult:
        cache_key = f"rca:{event.event_id}:{hashlib.md5(event.description.encode()).hexdigest()}"
        cached = self.cache.get_json(cache_key)
        if cached:
            return self._rca_from_dict(cached)

with traced_span("rca_analyze") as span:
            span.set_attribute("event.id", event.event_id)
            span.set_attribute("event.source_type", event.source_type)
            span.set_attribute("event.severity", event.severity)

query_text = f"{event.title}\n{event.description}\ncomponent={event.component}\nsource={event.source_type}"
            qvec = self.embedder.embed_query(
                query_text,
                metadata={
                    "release_id": event.release_id or "",
                    "component": event.component or "",
                    "severity": event.severity,
                },
            )

similar = self.index.search(
                qvec,
                limit=8,
                release_id=None,  # allow cross-release retrieval
                component=event.component,
            )

top_score = max([e.score for e in similar], default=0.0)

has_log = any(e.artifact_type == "log" for e in similar)
            has_metric = any(e.artifact_type == "metric" for e in similar)
            has_video = any(e.artifact_type == "video" for e in similar)

component_features = self.feast_client.get_component_features(event.component or "unknown")

# In real production this is a learned model with historical labels.
            cf = confidence_features(
                evidence_count=len(similar),
                top_similarity=top_score,
                has_log=has_log,
                has_metric=has_metric,
                has_video=has_video,
                data_completeness=0.8,
                signal_agreement=0.7,
                severity=event.severity,
            ).unsqueeze(0)

with torch.no_grad():
                confidence = torch.sigmoid(self.confidence_model(cf)).item()

# Add interpretable adjustment so demo is less random with untrained model.
            confidence = 0.5 * confidence + 0.5 * min(1.0, top_score + 0.2)

risk_score = self._risk_score(event, confidence, component_features)

hypothesis = RCAHypothesis(
                component=event.component or "unknown",
                hypothesis=self._generate_hypothesis_text(event, similar),
                confidence=float(confidence),
                evidence=similar[:5],
                counter_evidence=[
                    "Evidence is retrieval-based and should be confirmed by human triager.",
                    "If artifacts are incomplete, confidence should be downgraded.",
                ],
            )

result = RCAResult(
                event_id=event.event_id,
                summary=self._generate_summary(event, similar),
                hypotheses=[hypothesis],
                similar_artifacts=similar,
                risk_score=risk_score,
                recommended_actions=self._recommend_actions(event, risk_score, confidence),
                model_version="torch-demo-v1",
                created_at_ms=int(time.time() * 1000),
            )

self.cache.set_json(cache_key, self._rca_to_dict(result), ttl_sec=1800)
            return result

def _generate_summary(self, event: FailureEvent, similar: List[EvidenceRef]) -> str:
        if not similar:
            return f"No strong historical match found for event {event.event_id}. Human review required."

top = similar[0]
        return (
            f"Event {event.event_id} appears related to {event.component or 'unknown component'} "
            f"with top retrieved evidence from {top.artifact_type} artifact {top.artifact_id}. "
            f"This should be treated as an RCA hypothesis, not confirmed root cause."
        )

def _generate_hypothesis_text(self, event: FailureEvent, similar: List[EvidenceRef]) -> str:
        if not similar:
            return "Insufficient evidence to produce a specific root-cause hypothesis."

return (
            f"Likely issue in {event.component or 'unknown component'} based on semantic similarity "
            f"between the current failure description and historical artifacts. "
            f"Top evidence snippet: {similar[0].snippet[:200]}"
        )

def _risk_score(
        self,
        event: FailureEvent,
        confidence: float,
        component_features: Dict[str, Any],
    ) -> float:
        severity_base = {
            "S0": 90.0,
            "S1": 75.0,
            "S2": 45.0,
            "S3": 20.0,
        }.get(event.severity, 30.0)

recent_regression_rate = float(component_features.get("recent_regression_rate", 0.0))
        uncertainty_penalty = (1.0 - confidence) * 20.0
        regression_penalty = min(20.0, recent_regression_rate * 100.0)

return min(100.0, severity_base + uncertainty_penalty + regression_penalty)

def _recommend_actions(self, event: FailureEvent, risk_score: float, confidence: float) -> List[str]:
        actions = []

if risk_score >= 80:
            actions.append("Block or hold release until human triage confirms severity and owner.")
        elif risk_score >= 50:
            actions.append("Route to component owner and require review before release signoff.")
        else:
            actions.append("Track as low/medium risk; verify with standard triage workflow.")

if confidence < 0.6:
            actions.append("Collect more evidence or rerun scenario/test because confidence is low.")

actions.append("Attach retrieved evidence and ask human triager to accept, edit, or reject RCA.")
        return actions

def _rca_to_dict(self, r: RCAResult) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        return asdict(r)

def _rca_from_dict(self, d: Dict[str, Any]) -> RCAResult:
        similar = [EvidenceRef(**x) for x in d["similar_artifacts"]]
        hypotheses = []
        for h in d["hypotheses"]:
            hypotheses.append(
                RCAHypothesis(
                    component=h["component"],
                    hypothesis=h["hypothesis"],
                    confidence=h["confidence"],
                    evidence=[EvidenceRef(**x) for x in h["evidence"]],
                    counter_evidence=h["counter_evidence"],
                )
            )

return RCAResult(
            event_id=d["event_id"],
            summary=d["summary"],
            hypotheses=hypotheses,
            similar_artifacts=similar,
            risk_score=d["risk_score"],
            recommended_actions=d["recommended_actions"],
            model_version=d["model_version"],
            created_at_ms=d["created_at_ms"],
        )

## 9. Ray distributed processing

---

## ============================================================

def init_ray():
    try:
        import ray
        if not ray.is_initialized():
            ray.init(ignore_reinit_error=True)
        return ray
    except Exception:
        return None

RAY = init_ray()

def parse_log_text(text: str) -> str:
    """
    Basic log summarizer.
    Production:
    - structured log parser
    - stack trace extraction
    - secret redaction
    - error window extraction
    """
    lines = text.splitlines()
    interesting = [
        line for line in lines
        if any(k in line.lower() for k in ["error", "exception", "failed", "timeout", "assert"])
    ]
    return "\n".join(interesting[:20]) if interesting else "\n".join(lines[:10])

if RAY is not None:
    @RAY.remote
    def process_artifact_remote(raw: Dict[str, Any]) -> Artifact:
        text = raw.get("text", "")
        artifact_type = raw.get("artifact_type", "doc")

if artifact_type == "log":
            text = parse_log_text(text)

return Artifact(
            artifact_id=raw["artifact_id"],
            artifact_type=artifact_type,
            uri=raw["uri"],
            text=text,
            metadata=raw.get("metadata", {}),
        )

def process_artifacts_distributed(raw_artifacts: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Artifact]:
    """
    Use Ray when available; fallback to local processing.
    """
    if RAY is None:
        return [
            Artifact(
                artifact_id=r["artifact_id"],
                artifact_type=r.get("artifact_type", "doc"),
                uri=r["uri"],
                text=parse_log_text(r.get("text", "")) if r.get("artifact_type") == "log" else r.get("text", ""),
                metadata=r.get("metadata", {}),
            )
            for r in raw_artifacts
        ]

refs = [process_artifact_remote.remote(r) for r in raw_artifacts]
    return RAY.get(refs)

## 10. Ray Serve app

---

## ============================================================

def build_ray_serve_app(index_uri: str = "./.lancedb"):
    """
    Ray Serve model composition:
    - EmbeddingDeployment
    - RCADeployment with LanceDB retrieval and PyTorch confidence scoring

The Ray docs show @serve.deployment classes and serve.run(app).
    """

from fastapi import FastAPI
    from ray import serve

fastapi_app = FastAPI(title="Multimodal Release Intelligence API")

@serve.deployment(num_replicas=1)
    class RCADeployment:
        def __init__(self, index_uri: str):
            self.embedder = TorchEmbeddingService()
            self.index = LanceArtifactIndex(uri=index_uri)
            self.engine = RCAEngine(index=self.index, embedder=self.embedder)

@fastapi_app.post("/analyze")
        def analyze(self, event: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
            failure_event = FailureEvent(**event)
            result = self.engine.analyze(failure_event)
            return asdict(result)

@fastapi_app.post("/index")
        def index_artifacts(self, payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
            raw_artifacts = payload["artifacts"]
            artifacts = process_artifacts_distributed(raw_artifacts)
            self.index.upsert_artifacts(artifacts, self.embedder)
            return {"indexed": len(artifacts)}

@fastapi_app.get("/health")
        def health(self) -> Dict[str, str]:
            return {"status": "ok"}

@serve.deployment
    @serve.ingress(fastapi_app)
    class Ingress:
        def __init__(self, rca):
            self.rca = rca

return Ingress.bind(RCADeployment.bind(index_uri))

def demo():
    embedder = TorchEmbeddingService()
    index = LanceArtifactIndex(uri="./.lancedb_demo")
    engine = RCAEngine(index=index, embedder=embedder)

raw_artifacts = [
        {
            "artifact_id": "log-001",
            "artifact_type": "log",

            "text": """

            ERROR planner timeout after cost function update
            FAILED min_ttc below threshold
            stack: planner.compute_trajectory -> cost_model.score
            """,

            "metadata": {
                "release_id": "rc1",
                "component": "planner",
                "severity": "S1",
                "min_ttc": 0.42,
                "planner_latency_ms": 180,
            },
        },
        {
            "artifact_id": "video-caption-001",
            "artifact_type": "video",
            "uri": "s3://release/rc1/scenario-001.mp4",
            "text": "Unprotected left turn. Ego vehicle moves forward while oncoming vehicle approaches.",
            "metadata": {
                "release_id": "rc1",
                "component": "planner",
                "severity": "S1",
                "duration_sec": 30,
            },
        },
        {
            "artifact_id": "incident-182",
            "artifact_type": "doc",
            "uri": "jira://INC-182",
            "text": "Historical incident: planner cost function regression caused unsafe gap selection in unprotected left turn.",
            "metadata": {
                "release_id": "rc0",
                "component": "planner",
                "severity": "S1",
            },
        },
    ]

artifacts = process_artifacts_distributed(raw_artifacts)
    index.upsert_artifacts(artifacts, embedder)

event = FailureEvent(
        event_id="sim-failure-123",
        source_type="SIMULATION",
        release_id="rc1",
        component="planner",
        severity="S1",
        title="Simulation failure in unprotected left turn",
        description=(
            "Ego vehicle selected unsafe forward trajectory. "
            "min_ttc below threshold and planner latency increased."
        ),
        artifact_ids=["log-001", "metric-001", "video-caption-001"],
        metadata={"scenario_type": "unprotected_left", "odd": "night_urban"},
    )

result = engine.analyze(event)
    print(json.dumps(asdict(result), indent=2))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    demo()

# To run Ray Serve locally:
    #
    # from ray import serve
    # app = build_ray_serve_app("./.lancedb_demo")
    # serve.run(app, route_prefix="/")
    #
    # Then:
    # curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/analyze -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{...}'

5. How this connects to each resume stack#

5.1 Ray / Ray Serve#

在这个 project 里：

text
Ray Core:
- distributed artifact processing
- log parsing
- embedding batch jobs
- video/keyframe processing jobs

Ray Serve:
- online embedding service
- retrieval + RCA service
- model composition
- FastAPI endpoint

面试话术：

text
I used Ray for distributed artifact processing and Ray Serve for scalable online model serving. The important design is to separate batch artifact processing from online RCA serving. Logs, metrics, and video captions can be processed asynchronously; RCA serving can retrieve compact evidence and return structured results.

Ray Serve 官方文档示例展示了 @serve.deployment class、model composition、FastAPI integration，以及 serve.run(...) 部署方式；生产相关文档目录也包括 autoscaling、resource allocation、async inference、dynamic batching 等 topics，这些都正好对应你的 serving story。([Ray][1])

5.2 LanceDB / Milvus / Vector DB#

在这个 project 里：

text
LanceDB:
- stores artifact embeddings
- metadata filter by release_id/component/artifact_type
- vector search for similar incident retrieval

面试话术：

text
I used LanceDB in the prototype as the vector index. In a larger production system, the same abstraction could be backed by Milvus, OpenSearch vector, or another vector DB. The key idea is hybrid retrieval: semantic vector similarity plus metadata filters like release ID, component, severity, and artifact type.

LanceDB Python 文档里展示了本地或 object-storage URI 连接方式，也展示了 create_table(...) 和 table.search(...).where(...).limit(...).to_pandas() 的典型 retrieval pattern。([LanceDB][2])

5.3 PyTorch#

在这个 project 里：

text
PyTorch:
- MultimodalArtifactEncoder
- EvidenceReranker
- ConfidenceCalibrator

面试话术：

text
I would not rely only on generic LLM confidence. I would use PyTorch models for domain-specific embedding, reranking, and calibrated confidence. The embedding model learns similarity between failures or artifacts. The reranker selects the strongest evidence. The confidence model predicts whether a human triager is likely to accept the RCA.

你的 resume 里有 PyTorch、CUDA、Triton、Ray Serve、vLLM 这些 ML infra 技术栈，非常适合这个说法。

5.4 Feast#

在这个 project 里：

text
Feast:
- component health features
- historical regression rate
- owner acceptance rate
- historical fix time
- flaky test rate

面试话术：

text
Feast is useful when the RCA or risk model needs online features that are consistent with offline training. For example, component-level regression rate, historical fix time, flaky test rate, owner acceptance rate, or release health features can be used by the confidence/risk model.

Feast 官方 quickstart 展示了 FeatureStore(repo_path=".") 和 get_online_features(...) 的 online feature retrieval workflow。([Feast][3])

5.5 Redis#

在这个 project 里：

text
Redis:
- cache RCA result by event hash
- cache hot triage state
- avoid repeated expensive LLM/model calls

面试话术：

text
Redis is not the source of truth. It is for hot state and cost control: repeated RCA result cache, active triage queue, dashboard counters, rate limiting, and model response cache.

5.6 OpenTelemetry#

在这个 project 里：

text
OpenTelemetry:
- span around embedding
- span around RCA analysis
- attributes for event_id/source/severity

面试话术：

text
For production AI infra, observability is not optional. I would trace every major step: artifact fetch, parse, embedding, vector search, reranking, LLM generation, confidence scoring, and human feedback. Then I would track both system metrics and AI-quality metrics.

OpenTelemetry Python docs define instrumentation as adding observability code, show SDK setup with TracerProvider, and show creating spans and attributes, which matches the pattern in the prototype.([OpenTelemetry][4])

7. Interview answer: “connect the dots”#

这个你可以直接背：

text
The way I connect the stack is:

Kafka/Flink ingest events and artifacts continuously. Ray handles distributed artifact processing and batch embedding generation. PyTorch models generate multimodal embeddings, rerank retrieved evidence, and calibrate confidence. LanceDB or Milvus stores artifact embeddings and supports similar-incident retrieval with metadata filters. Feast provides online features for risk scoring and owner prediction. Ray Serve exposes embedding, retrieval, reranking, and RCA services. Redis caches hot RCA results and active triage state. OpenTelemetry traces every step so we can debug latency, failures, and AI quality.

For adt, the entities are creative assets, media artifacts, semantic tags, and discovery events. For wolf, the entities are simulation failures, CI logs, on-road incidents, release candidates, and RCA evidence. The platform architecture is the same: multimodal ingestion, indexing, retrieval, model serving, workflow orchestration, observability, and feedback.

8. The “senior” design points to emphasize#

text
1. Raw artifacts stay in object storage.
   LLM/model gets compact evidence packets, not giant raw logs/videos.

2. Vector search is not enough.
   Need hybrid retrieval: exact match + vector + metadata + reranking.

3. Confidence is a model/system property.
   Do not trust LLM self-confidence.

4. Ray processing and Ray Serve should be separated.
   Batch artifact processing is not the same as online RCA serving.

5. Redis is cache, not source of truth.

6. Feast is useful only when online/offline feature consistency matters.
   Do not force it everywhere.

7. Observability must include AI quality metrics:
   acceptance rate, hallucination rate, citation correctness, confidence calibration.

8. Human feedback is training/eval data.
   Accept/edit/reject RCA becomes future evaluation signal.

9. How to frame this as your resume project#

你可以这样说：

text
In my resume, the adt AI Infra work can be explained as a generalized multimodal AI workflow platform. I built the shared execution layer for onboarding, retrieval, generation, semantic tagging, and assistant-style workloads.

If I were to explain it using this reference architecture:
- Ray maps to distributed workflow execution and model serving.
- LanceDB/Milvus maps to multimodal retrieval and semantic search.
- PyTorch maps to embeddings, ranking, and confidence models.
- Feast maps to online feature consistency for model decisions.
- Redis maps to hot cache and active workflow state.
- OpenTelemetry/Prometheus/Grafana map to production observability.
- The unified intent registry and execution planner map to routing requests across retrieval, inference, LLM generation, and policy checks.

This ties directly to your adt AI Infra bullet: you built a context-aware AI infra platform for multimodal and LLM-powered workflows, with unified intent registry, execution planner, artifact references, policy checks, model dependencies, runtime selection, and rollout controls.

10. One sentence summary#

This project turns your resume from a list of tools into one coherent platform story: multimodal artifacts come in, Ray processes them, PyTorch embeds/ranks/scores them, LanceDB retrieves similar evidence, Feast adds online features, Ray Serve exposes the workflow, Redis caches hot state, and OpenTelemetry makes it production-debuggable.
